# M7: The PC's Sensors on the Board, Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** M7 (spec §18, D44) on the board: a sync finds the owner's PC running `reflbo-stats`, a session switches to sync mode `always` and the PC preset as the owner set it and gives back only what the owner hasn't changed when the PC goes quiet; its readings are `pc.<id>` fields; every numeric field can show as a history chart, a bar, a ring or a gauge, per slot and cell; a PC layout and preset; the PC page, Show as in the editor, `pc status` and `pc demo`.

**Architecture:**

- **Pure logic, host-tested:** `pcstats` (new): the PC app's messages and the PC step's outcome (`pc_msg`), the session's rules (`pc_session`). `ui`: a number on every numeric value; `pc.<id>` fields and the presets' table of reading ids (`ui_pc`); the charts' history (`ui_history`); the four visuals (`ui_visual`); visuals in `presets.json` and on the dashboard; the PC layout and preset, the status bar's monitor and "PC asleep"; the catalogue's fields and rules for the page (`ui_catalog`). `storage`: the `pc` settings, the token and the PC step. `sync_plan`: the PC step never fails a sync.
- **Device side:** `pcstats`' WebSocket (`pc_ws`, `esp_websocket_client` 1.8.0); `sync`'s PC step and the PC page's Test; `main/app_pc.c`, the session on the app task, with its hooks in `app.c`, `app_ui.c`, `app_sync.c`, `app_menu.c`, `app_cmds.c` and `app_web.c`; the presets in a block of their own in RTC FAST memory.
- **Web:** the PC page, Show as in the preset editor, the PC's readings in its field lists, the PC step on the Sync page.

**Tech stack:** ESP-IDF v5.5.5, cJSON, LittleFS, `espressif/esp_websocket_client` 1.8.0 (new, from the registry); Unity on the host; Node's test runner for the page. The other side is `reflbo-stats` (Go, `~/reflbo-stats-win`), with its own plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-19-m7-reflbo-stats.md` there).

**Spec:** `docs/specs/2026-09-25-firmware-design.md` r53 (D44, written 2026-10-19 from the owner's answers that day; the owner approved the visuals and the PC layout from the spike's renders: "looks great!"). Task 14 brings it to r54, as built.
- Relevant: §1.2's D44; §5.1 (`pc.<id>`), §5.2 (the monitor, the PC layout), §5.3 (Show as, the visuals, History), §5.4 (Visuals, the built-in PC preset), §5.7 (Sync ▸ PC), §6 (RTC memory), §9.3 (the PC step), §10.3 (the PC page, Show as), §12a (the protocol and the session), §14.2 (`pc_token`), §15 (`pc status`, `pc demo`), §17, §18.
- Also `AGENTS.md` §3.4 (gotchas 22, 25, 29, 30, 33, 43), §5.3, §6 (the stable firmware and board tests), §7, §8; the PC app's `docs/spec.md` (its §6, the LAN API).

**Research behind this plan (2026-10-19):**
- **How it was built.** The plan's code was built and tested task by task on the branch `plan/m7pc` from main (2b32412), one commit per task (`plan-m7pc task N: …`); the plan carries that code. A review of the whole branch and its fixes are folded into those commits (below). The PC app's plan ran first, on `plan/m7` in `~/reflbo-stats-win`, and this plan's messages, limits and close codes follow what that branch sends.
- **Tests and sizes as built.** On the host the whole suite passed after every task: 77 ctest targets at the end (69 before: `test_pcstats`, `test_pc_session`, `test_ui_pc`, `test_ui_history`, `test_ui_visual`, `test_ui_visual_golden`, `test_ui_preset_visuals` and `test_ui_pc_layout` are new), and the page's 86 tests (73 before); the PC's messages and session, the history, the presets and the PC fields passed under ASan and UBSan. 16 goldens are new: 10 sheets of the visuals (`vis_*`) and 6 dashboards (`dash_classic_visuals`, `dash_split_visuals`, `dash_pc_session`, `dash_pc_new`, `dash_pc_asleep`, `dash_pc_asleep_cs`). The firmware built clean, without a warning: 2 607 536 bytes at the end (2 548 560 before), 38 % of the app slot free. The RTC snapshot's `.rtc.data` went from 6 960 bytes to 4 120, as the presets left it for a block of their own in RTC FAST memory: 4 440 bytes of its 4 608 (`.rtc.force_fast` 4 444). The largest `presets.json` is 54 243 bytes of its 64 KB. The charts' history takes 761 KB of PSRAM once a preset charts a field.
- **What was there to build on.** `fetch_get()` with a bearer token (M6d's SolaX Developer API) for the PC step's `/v1/info`; the sync's steps with their switches and a failure that never fails the sync (D35, D36); D42's titles and their pool in the presets, which the visuals ride; the split layout's cells (M6b, M6c) and the value widgets' sizes; `util_json_depth()` (gotcha 30); the Solar page's Check now as a sync of its own kind, which the PC page's Test copies; D43's last-sync time, whose form "PC asleep" reuses.
- **Not checked on the board:** everything the device does is Task 14's: the PC step against the PC app's demo on this Mac, a session's start and end with KEY and BOOT double, Quit and a frozen app, a wrong token, the page, a restart during a session, a deep sleep's warm wake from RTC FAST, `pc demo`, a night during a session, the heap and the stacks.
- **The review (2026-10-19, opus):** a fresh reviewer read the whole branch before this plan was generated, cross-checked it against the PC app's branch, ran the suites and a clean firmware build, measured the RTC sections and the app task's deepest chart render with `-fstack-usage` (about 4.1 KB of the 8 KB stack), and confirmed that IDF 5.5.5 keeps RTC FAST memory through deep sleep. No Critical issue.
  - **Important, fixed:**
    - A session's switch to `always` went into `settings.json`, and the first catalogue's fill saved `presets.json` with the session's preset active, so a reboot during a session (an update, a power cycle) left the board in `always` for good, and one after it on the PC preset (the files now keep the owner's mode and preset while a session runs).
    - The PC app's Quit and its refusals left the board waiting about 15 s, where the PC app's spec promises its Quit ends sessions at once (the close's code and reason are read, `pc_close_action()`; a refused reconnect says "wrong token"). Then found while writing Task 14: the PC app closes with the same code, 1001, when the board's pongs go missing, which now waits for the reconnect.
    - A schedule's night entry during a session was dropped, not deferred, so a late session kept the board awake all night (it now starts at the session's end, while its end is ahead).
    - `pc_ws_close()` held the app task for a second or two at each reconnect to a sleeping PC, seven or more while it connected to a name (a task of its own closes and frees the connection now).
    - A deliberate flash back to `stable-m6d` loses the owner's presets, which it refuses with M7's layout, fields and size (Task 14 keeps a pre-M7 backup and notes it in `AGENTS.md` §6).
  - **Re-graded Important, fixed:** a value of 1e39 drew as "inf" and a `seq` of 1e20 was an undefined cast (left out and clamped now); the PC step's report applied after the sync was marked done, so a nested refresh could overwrite it and lose a Sync now's toast.
  - **Fixed with them, in the same code:** values more than 24.8 days old read as new in the charts (an unsigned age now); fragmented WebSocket messages were never put together; every routine deep-sleep wake zeroed the history's 761 KB (taken once the board stays awake now); the editor showed a slot whose reading the PC no longer sends as empty (kept as "pc.<id> (not sent now)"); the largest file's test now has bounds at their longest (54 243 bytes); the bar label's ruling now says three quarters, as the code does.
  - **Left for later, in spec r54 §20 (Task 14):**
    - The editor's bounds for a power or an energy are in kW and kWh and don't say so, while the text shows W under 1 kW (D38).
    - `pc demo off` empties the PC preset even when the owner changed it meanwhile; `pc demo on` during a session says "shows" and does nothing; a session that starts during the demo keeps its fill and stale values.
    - The PC step can take longer than its 2 s: the timeout bounds the connect and each read, not a name's lookup, and a reply whose body times out reads as "not reflbo-stats".
    - RTC FAST memory no longer has room for M8's values block, which spec §6 and §20 kept there; RTC slow memory has about 4 KB free, about M8's estimate (Task 14 writes it down for M8's refresh).

**Rulings this plan makes (each costs little if wrong):**

- **The whole presets block moves to RTC FAST memory** (Task 3), not only the visuals' pool as spec §6 has it: the snapshot in RTC slow memory had 6 832 of its 7 KB cap with D42's titles, and the presets (with the titles, the PC ids and the visuals) fit RTC FAST's free 8 KB whole. One block, its own version and CRC; a warm wake needs both blocks valid.
- **A PC reading is a field byte 128 + n** (Task 5), n an entry in a 32-entry table of reading ids kept with the presets (`ui_presets_t.pc_ids`, 512 bytes in the RTC FAST block). A slot keeps its reading whatever the catalogue's order, and a renamed reading shows its old id with "—" (spec §12a.3). The table is rebuilt from the file at each parse, in the order the file names the ids.
- **presets.json may reach 64 KB** (Task 5; it was 48 KB): 24 cells a preset of PC readings with 15-character ids make the largest file 49 870 bytes, and the visuals (Task 8) add more. The web server's body and reply buffers grow to 72 KB with it, in PSRAM.
- **A PC reading's icon** (Task 5) is the thermometer for °C and °F and a computer (Material `computer`) for the rest; the spec names none. Pinned by the goldens of Task 7.
- **A PC reading's range** is the catalogue's only when both `min` and `max` came and `min < max`; one alone counts as none (automatic).
- **A PC value past what a 32-bit long holds** with its decimals drops them, and past that prints whole with `%.0f`.
- **The history keeps each value's time** (Task 6): 3 600 values with their times and 1 440 minutes with their minimum, mean, maximum and a break flag, about 47 KB a series where the spec estimated 32 KB, 761 KB for all 16 in PSRAM. Without the times a session's gap would draw as a straight line between the values on either side of it.
- **A break** (Task 6) is a value no number holds, pushed when a source's run is interrupted (a reconnect, a `null`); the chart doesn't join the columns on either side. A minute that holds a break is marked whole, so the line stops at that minute's column even when the break came after its values.
- **An age is the unsigned difference to now** (Task 6, the review's): a time up to 60 s ahead of now counts as now, anything further as old, so values weeks old (a board on USB with the PC off for a month) never read as new, as a signed 32-bit age made them after 24.8 days.
- **The values cover a span** while their oldest's age plus one mean interval reaches it, or while the ring isn't full yet; past that the chart draws from the minutes (spec §5.3: "A span longer than the last 3 600 values cover draws from the minutes"). A day without a value clears the minutes.
- **A series is named by its field's name in presets.json** ("pc.cpu_load"), not by its field byte, which a new presets file can give to another reading.
- **The chart's line slopes from value to value** (Task 7), each at its own time, where the spike's made-up series drew one step a column; the light fill follows the line across the columns between values, and a break leaves them apart.
- **A bar, a ring or a gauge with no range of its own or of its field** (Task 7) runs from 0 to the round number (1, 2 or 5 times a power of ten) past the value, or from the one below it to 0 for a negative value. The editor asks for both bounds there (Task 13).
- **A chart's automatic bounds** (Task 7) are the values in view with a tenth's room, on a tenth of a round step (52-72 °C reads 50-75), where the spike floored and ceiled with half a unit more: a small range such as 1.21-1.25 V keeps its decimals.
- **A visual's number** is the resolved value's text, with the language's decimal comma; as the spike did, it gives up its unit before its decimals ("3,4" in a small gauge).
- **A bar's label comes first** (at most three quarters of the width), its value in the rest; the spike had no cap, and a small cell's value then kept no room.
- **Two things the spike didn't show** (Task 7): a gauge's range ends stay inside a narrow cell, and a gauge in a tall cell sits in its middle.
- **A chart reads its history 50 columns at a time** (Task 7) on the app task's stack, so `ui` needs no PSRAM attribute of its own.
- **The visuals reach the renderer with the titles** (Task 8): `ui_titles_t` carries each slot's visual beside its title, so `ui_draw_dashboard()` keeps its signature and its 18 callers; its comment now says "a preset's own settings for its slots".
- **The visuals' pool** (Task 8) has 48 entries of 20 bytes (the preset, the slot and a 16-byte visual), 960 bytes where the spec estimated 580; the presets block is 4 440 of its 4 608 bytes in RTC FAST memory.
- **A visual that doesn't fit its slot or cell draws the value** (Task 8), as the field would without it; the file isn't refused for it, as the slot sizes are the layout's and the editor offers only the visuals that fit (Task 13).
- **A chart's `numbers` are on unless the file turns them off** (Task 8), as in the approved PC layout; a chart is always written with its `span_s` and `numbers`. A bound is written with its float's own digits (`%.7g`), so 23.7 stays 23.7.
- **A power's and an energy's bounds are in kW and kWh** (Task 8), the fields' base units, though their text shows W under 1 kW (D38): the number a visual draws is the field's `num`. Spec §5.4's "in the field's unit" reads as that base unit.
- **The fields a visual takes** are the numbers, the battery and the levels (`aq.index`, `aq.uv`), the kinds that carry a `num` (Task 4); the pollen, the forecast's chart and the flow don't.
- **The built-in PC preset is offered with its slots empty** (Task 9) and filled, once, by `ui_presets_fill_pc()` when the first catalogue comes: the first four readings as 60 s charts with their numbers, the next three as bars, each with the reading's own range. A visual is never kept on an empty slot, so the file always reads back.
- **"PC asleep" draws the charts empty** (Task 9), their labels and baselines only, as the approved render had them, though the history is still in RAM; elsewhere a PC chart keeps its history after the session, with "—" for its value. A chart with neither a value nor a history in view draws its label and baseline only, everywhere.
- **"PC asleep"'s second line** (Task 9) is "since <time> · <name>" with the time in D43's form (today `18:12`, earlier `Mon 18:12`, else the date), each part left out when unknown: before any session since the boot it says "PC asleep" alone. Czech: "PC spí", "od 18:12".
- **The status bar's monitor** is the spike's hand-drawn 14×12 mark (Task 9), after the globe; the new `pc` icon (Material `computer`) is the readings' symbol in the value widgets.
- **A new layout id goes last** (`UI_LAYOUT_PC`, Task 9), so the presets in RTC memory and in files keep their layouts.
- **The PC step runs for a file from before M7** (Task 10): `sync.steps` lists the steps that run, and an older list never names "pc", so a file without a `pc` object turns the step on; M7 writes the object whenever it saves, after which the list says it.
- **The PC's settings take each bad value's default** (Task 10), as the other blocks do: a host that isn't a name or an address, a port outside 1-65535, a preset that isn't an id. The interval is clamped to 250 ms-10 s.
- **The token takes letters, digits, dashes and spaces** (Task 10), 63 at most, kept as typed (spec §12a.1); anything else is refused with the reason, as the other secrets are.
- **Menu ▸ Sync ▸ Steps gains PC** (Task 10), the step's switch; Menu ▸ Sync ▸ PC with the session's state comes with the device's side (Task 12).
- **The PC step's outcome is pure logic** (`pc_probe_result()`, Task 11), host-tested: a reply's status and body give "found" (with "HWiNFO …" when HWiNFO isn't well), "off" (no answer) or a refusal ("wrong token", "HTTP 503", "protocol 2", "not reflbo-stats"). In the report: found is OK, off is not run, a refusal fails the step and never the sync.
- **"off" means two things on the PC step** (Task 11), as spec §9.3 words it: the step switched off (D35's word for every step) and the PC not answering. Both are "not run"; the page shows the step's switch beside it.
- **PC stats off, or no address, skips the step** with "no PC" or "no address" (Task 11), before any request.
- **The WebSocket client doesn't reconnect by itself** (`disable_auto_reconnect`, Task 11): the session reconnects once and then ends (spec §12a.3). Each connection has its own buffer in PSRAM, where a message is put together from its fragments, at most 8 KB, before it leaves the client's task; a larger one is reported for the session to close. A close is told once, with the PC app's code and reason, or the upgrade's HTTP status when it was refused.
- **`pc_ws_close()` never waits** (Task 11, the review's): a task of its own (`pc_ws_close`, 3 KB of stack, priority 2) closes the connection and destroys the client, which can take a second or two at a reconnect to a PC gone to sleep, longer when it is still connecting to a name; the app task treats the stream as gone at once, and an old connection's late events carry its generation, which the session ignores.
- **A number no float holds is no value** (Task 1, the review's): a reading or a bound past ±3.4e38 is left out like a `null`, rather than drawn as "inf"; a sequence past 2³²−1 and a protocol past an int are clamped before they are cast.
- **The snapshot's version goes to 17** (Task 11): the syncs' state keeps a result and a detail for each step, and the PC step adds one.
- **A schedule's night entry waits for the PC session's end** (Tasks 2 and 12, the review's): spec §12a.4 keeps the schedule's entries from running during a session, and a night skipped outright left the board awake all night after a late session. The session keeps the latest such night's end; when it ends and has given back what it changed, the night starts until then, if that is still ahead, with the toast "Night until 06:00". The console's `night` is the owner's own hand, and ends the session first, so what it changed comes back before the deep sleep loses the session's memory.
- **The files never keep a session's switches** (Tasks 2 and 12, the review's): while a session runs, `settings.json` is written with the sync mode before the session's `always`, and `presets.json` names the preset before the session's as active while the session's is on screen (the first catalogue's fill saves the file in the middle of a session). A restart, an update, a crash or a power cycle are cold boots, which read the files, so the board comes back as the owner left it and never stays in `always` on battery. A change the owner makes by hand is saved as made.
- **PC stats switched off, or a new address or port, end a session** at the next tick (Task 12); a new token takes effect at the next session, as NVS changes reach no running connection.
- **A stream the PC app closes on purpose ends the session at once** (Tasks 11 and 12, the review's; spec §12a.3 says nothing of close codes, the PC app's §8 promises it of Quit): Quit's 1001, a 1008 refusal ("new token", "protocol 2", "no hello", "too deep") and a refused upgrade (401 "wrong token", another status as "HTTP 503"), as `pc_close_action()` decides, host-tested. A connection lost without a word, and the app's 1001 "no pong" (the board's answers to its pings lost, a network's hiccup), wait for the silence rule: the reconnect after three intervals (5 s at least), the end about 15 s after the last message.
- **The history's memory is taken when the presets first chart a field** (Task 12), once the board stays awake (at `come_alive()` and at each change of the presets, never on a routine deep-sleep wake, which would zero 761 KB for nothing: deep sleep loses PSRAM), and kept; the PC's values go in with each message (a `null` as a break), the indoor values and the battery at each sample, every other field at each sync and each refresh of the house's reading.
- **`pc demo on` fills an empty PC preset for its while** (Task 12) and empties it again at `pc demo off`, so the sample's readings never take the place of the PC app's first catalogue. A reboot during the demo keeps the filled preset.
- **The PC step's report is applied before the sync lets a new one start** (Task 12, the review's): a session the report starts switches the mode, which reschedules; applied after, a nested refresh could overwrite the report and lose a Sync now's toast.
- **The catalogue's cache is read once storage is up** (Task 12): a cold boot reads it at once, a warm wake at its first tick with storage mounted.
- **Menu ▸ Sync ▸ PC** (Task 12): PC stats on or off, and the session's state: the PC's name and the last message's age, "since 18:12" after one, the last PC step's detail ("off", "wrong token"), or "None yet".
- **The PC page's Test is a sync of its own kind** (`SYNC_KIND_PC`, Task 13): the PC step alone, joining Wi-Fi if need be, with no mark in the status bar and no history, as the Solar page's Check now. A PC it finds starts a session as a sync's step would: the owner asked.
- **`GET /api/pc`** (Task 13): the session (on, connected, the demo, whether it switched to always, the PC's name, HWiNFO, its preset, since, the last message's age, the last trouble), the catalogue's readings, the last sync's PC step and the last Test; never the token. `GET /api/settings` says in `pc.keys.token` whether one is set.
- **`GET /api/fields` lists the PC's readings** (Task 13) from the session's or the cached catalogue, as `pc.<id>` in the group "pc", and every field says whether a visual takes it (`numeric`) and its own range (`min`, `max`). `GET /api/layouts` publishes each visual's least room, as `ui_visual_fits()` decides it, and a chart's spans.
- **A reading the PC no longer sends stays in the editor** (Task 13, the review's) as "pc.<id> (not sent now)", with its Show as, so a save from the page never drops a slot the file holds.
- **The editor's Show as** (Task 13) offers only the visuals with room in the slot or cell; an empty bound is the field's own (its range as the placeholder), and a chart's may be "auto"; the span is a list from 10 s to 24 h, with the file's own value kept when it isn't on it. A visual goes when its field stops being a number, travels with its field on Split and Join, and goes when a ratio leaves it no room, with a toast.
- **Set aside, as the review declined to judge them:**
  - the board's behaviour on the hardware (warm wakes from RTC FAST, timing, power): Task 14's checks and the owner's acceptance;
  - the visuals' fine look, the chart's slopes in place of the spike's steps among them: the owner sees `dash_pc_session` at the acceptance;
  - the PC app's escaping, which its own §12 notes can push a catalogue past 8 KB: the board refuses such a frame with its reason, and the PC app's plan owns it;
  - an IPv6 literal as the PC's host (the URL has no brackets): the spec asks for none;
  - a ninth board, which the PC app refuses with 503: one owner has one board, and a refusal ends that session at once;
  - the client library's own pings every 10 s beside the app's 5 s: the board answers the app's, which decide;
  - Menu ▸ Sync ▸ PC's session line while the menu stays open: it is read at each key press, as Info's uptime is, not with each message; the menu redraws on keys only.

## Global Constraints

- ESP-IDF **v5.5.x** (v5.5.5), target `esp32s3`. C17 firmware, plain HTML/CSS/JS in `web/` with no build step and no external resources.
- `[host]` code (`pcstats`' `pc_msg.c` and `pc_session.c`, all of `ui`, `storage`'s settings, `sync_plan.c`) includes no ESP-IDF headers. cJSON counts as plain C.
- ESP-IDF style: 4-space indent, `snake_case`, a component or module prefix on public APIs, public headers in `include/`. C lines stay within 120 characters; the page's files keep their own style.
- The app task owns the presets, the display, storage and the session (spec §3.2, `AGENTS.md` §5.3); the stream's task and the sync's hand over through `app_post()` and never block on the app; console commands that touch them run on it through `diag_on_owner()`.
- **The RTC snapshot is at most 7 KB, the presets block at most 4 608 bytes** (`_Static_assert`s in `main/app.c`); a change to either's layout bumps its version.
- **Input from outside is checked before cJSON recurses** (gotcha 30): a PC frame is at most 8 KB and 4 levels deep; `presets.json` keeps its 20 levels.
- **GCC sees truncation clang doesn't** (gotcha 33): size buffers for the widest value their format prints.
- **Never commit or log secrets**: the PC's token is write-only, stays in NVS (`secrets/pc_token`), never in a backup, a log or a reply.
- **Board tests** (memory `board-test-protocol`): back up the configuration first (`GET /api/backup`) and restore it after (`POST /api/restore`), then compare. Never erase flash, NVS or the storage partition without asking. Board commands go through `tools/idf.sh` with an explicit `-p`; the port must be confirmed as this board (USB serial `14:C1:9F:54:BB:94`, `flash` prints `MAC: 14:c1:9f:54:bb:94`). The web password may be reset from the menu at any time (the owner, 2026-10-02); a temporary one is set over the device's own network and cleared again at the end.
- List every component source in `SRCS`. One new dependency from the registry, pinned: `espressif/esp_websocket_client` `==1.8.0`; commit `dependencies.lock`.
- Small, focused Conventional Commits that each build. Push to `origin` freely; never force-push `main`. **No attribution of any kind: no `Co-Authored-By` or other trailer in any commit** (the owner's rule, memory `no-commit-trailers`).
- Build only what the spec covers (r53: D44). Not in M7: the app on macOS or Linux, several PCs, a session started from a button (spec §19's proposals), MQTT and Home Assistant (M8).

## Review Focus

1. **The PC going away and coming back during a session**: the PC sleeps, the app quits, Wi-Fi drops, HWiNFO stops or goes stale, the app restarts with a new catalogue or a renamed reading. Expected: the app's Quit or refusal ending the session at once; a silence, or a lost pong, bringing one reconnect after three intervals (5 s at least) and the end about 15 s after the last message; what the session changed given back only where the owner hasn't changed it, a renamed reading's slots showing its old id with "—", the charts' lines broken, nothing left over across many sessions. Pinned by:
   - `test_pcstats`' close codes (Task 11); `test_pc_session`'s silence, reconnect and end (Task 2); `test_ui_pc`'s renamed reading and its freshness (Task 5); `test_ui_history`'s breaks and old values (Task 6);
   - Task 14 Steps 6 and 9, the PC app stopped and started on this Mac.
2. **The owner's hand during a session**: KEY, the menu's preset, BOOT double, a page changing the sync mode or the PC settings, PC stats off, the schedule's entries and nights, the console's `night`, quiet hours, a critical battery. Expected: spec §12a.4, Wi-Fi kept while the session runs, a schedule's night starting at the session's end, never a sync mode `always` left behind that the owner didn't choose, not even by a reboot in the middle of a session. Pinned by:
   - `test_pc_session`'s ends after KEY and BOOT double, the owner's own `always`, the cycle's pause, the files' own mode and preset, the deferred night (Task 2);
   - Task 14 Steps 6, 9 and 10b: KEY and BOOT double during sessions, a restart during one, a night during one.
3. **A broken or hostile stream**: frames over 8 KB, JSON deeper than 4, a catalogue of 33 readings or with a repeated id, readings for unknown ids, NaN or 1e39, `null`s, messages every 250 ms. Expected: refused or closed with the reason, no crash, the app task never blocked, the board's queue never jammed. Pinned by:
   - `test_pcstats`'s refusals, the frame's size and depth, a catalogue refused whole (Task 1); `test_ui_pc`'s huge values (Task 5);
   - Task 14 Step 5's heap and stacks during a session.
4. **Presets with PC fields and visuals through every path**: the page's save and preview, a backup and its restore, a deep sleep's RTC FAST block and a reboot, the largest file, the first catalogue filling the PC preset, `pc demo`. Expected: each round trips; the preview draws the posted file's own readings; an owner's PC preset is never refilled. Pinned by:
   - `test_ui_pc` and `test_ui_preset_visuals`' round trips and refusals, the largest file (Tasks 5, 8); `test_ui_pc_layout`'s fill (Task 9); the page's tests (Task 13);
   - Task 14 Steps 8, 9, 10 and 11.
5. **Memory and time on the board**: the history's 761 KB of PSRAM, four charts redrawn every second, the app task's 8 KB stack, the RTC FAST block at 4 440 of its 4 608 bytes, the web server's 72 KB buffers, the PC step's 2 s in a sync's 45 s. Expected: a session that runs for hours without the heap shrinking, a redraw well inside the interval, every buffer within its limit. Pinned by:
   - `test_ui_history`'s chunks and wrap (Task 6), `test_ui_visual`'s cells (Task 7), the presets block's `_Static_assert` (Task 3);
   - Task 14 Step 5's `heap` and `tasks`.

---

### Task 1: The PC app's messages (`pcstats`)

**Files:**
- Create: `components/pcstats/CMakeLists.txt`, `components/pcstats/include/pc_msg.h`, `components/pcstats/pc_msg.c`, `test/host/test_pcstats.c`
- Modify: `test/host/CMakeLists.txt` (the `pcstats_logic` library and the test)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `util_json_depth()` (`util`), cJSON.
- Produces (`pc_msg.h`):
  - the limits `PC_PROTOCOL` (1), `PC_READINGS_MAX` (32), `PC_ID_LEN` (16), `PC_LABEL_LEN` (24), `PC_UNIT_LEN` (8), `PC_FRAME_MAX` (8192), `PC_DEPTH_MAX` (4), `PC_INTERVAL_MIN_MS`, `PC_INTERVAL_MAX_MS`;
  - `pc_hwinfo_t` (`PC_HW_OK`, `PC_HW_NOT_RUNNING`, `PC_HW_SHM_OFF`, `PC_HW_STALE`, `PC_HW_UNKNOWN`), `pc_reading_t`, `pc_catalog_t`, `pc_values_t` (`seq`, `has[]`, `v[]`), `pc_info_t`, `pc_msg_kind_t`;
  - `size_t pc_hello(char *out, size_t size, uint32_t interval_ms)`;
  - `pc_msg_kind_t pc_parse(const char *json, size_t len, pc_catalog_t *cat, pc_values_t *vals, pc_hwinfo_t *hw, char *err, size_t err_size)`;
  - `bool pc_parse_info(const char *json, size_t len, pc_info_t *info, char *err, size_t err_size)`;
  - `size_t pc_catalog_json(const pc_catalog_t *cat, char *out, size_t size)`;
  - `int pc_catalog_find(const pc_catalog_t *cat, const char *id)`;
  - `pc_hwinfo_t pc_hwinfo_by_name(const char *)`, `const char *pc_hwinfo_name(pc_hwinfo_t)`.

The messages are spec §12a.3's, as `reflbo-stats` sends them (its `internal/server/lan.go` and `lan_test.go` pin the same shapes):
- a catalogue is taken whole or not at all, so the fields never see half of one;
- readings go by id, and a `null` or a missing id is no value;
- a frame over 8 KB, JSON deeper than 4 levels, an id outside 1–15 of a-z, 0-9 and _, a repeated id, more than 32 readings, decimals outside 0–3 are refused with the reason;
- a number no float holds (past ±3.4e38) is no value, as a `null` is; a sequence past 2³²−1 and a protocol past an int are clamped before they are cast, so no conversion is undefined;
- an unknown `type` and unknown keys are ignored;
- labels and units are cut at a UTF-8 character.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.**

`test/host/test_pcstats.c`:

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "pc_msg.h"
#include "unity.h"

/* The PC app's messages (spec §12a, D44): the board's hello, the app's catalogue, readings and status, and
 * /v1/info, as reflbo-stats sends them (its server tests pin the same shapes). */

static pc_catalog_t s_cat;
static pc_values_t s_vals;
static char s_err[96];
static char s_buf[9000];

void setUp(void)
{
    memset(&s_cat, 0, sizeof(s_cat));
    memset(&s_vals, 0, sizeof(s_vals));
    s_err[0] = '\0';
}

void tearDown(void) {}

static pc_msg_kind_t parse(const char *json, pc_hwinfo_t *hw)
{
    pc_hwinfo_t dummy;
    return pc_parse(json, strlen(json), &s_cat, &s_vals, hw ? hw : &dummy, s_err, sizeof(s_err));
}

static const char k_catalog[] =
    "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":["
    "{\"id\":\"cpu_load\",\"label\":\"CPU load\",\"unit\":\"%\",\"decimals\":0,\"min\":0,\"max\":100},"
    "{\"id\":\"cpu_temp\",\"label\":\"CPU\",\"unit\":\"\\u00b0C\",\"decimals\":1},"
    "{\"id\":\"ram_used\",\"label\":\"RAM\",\"unit\":\"GB\",\"decimals\":1,\"min\":0,\"max\":64,\"extra\":true}]}";

static void test_the_hello_names_the_protocol_and_the_interval(void)
{
    char out[96];
    size_t n = pc_hello(out, sizeof(out), 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("{\"type\":\"hello\",\"protocol\":1,\"interval_ms\":1000}", out);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(strlen(out), n);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, pc_hello(out, 10, 1000)); /* too small: nothing */
}

static void test_the_catalogue_is_read_with_its_ranges(void)
{
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_CATALOG, parse(k_catalog, NULL));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(3, s_cat.count);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("cpu_load", s_cat.r[0].id);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("CPU load", s_cat.r[0].label);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("%", s_cat.r[0].unit);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(s_cat.r[0].has_min && s_cat.r[0].has_max);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_FLOAT(100.0f, s_cat.r[0].max);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("\xc2\xb0" "C", s_cat.r[1].unit);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(1, s_cat.r[1].decimals);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s_cat.r[1].has_min || s_cat.r[1].has_max);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(2, pc_catalog_find(&s_cat, "ram_used"));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(-1, pc_catalog_find(&s_cat, "gone"));
}

static void test_readings_go_by_id_and_null_is_no_value(void)
{
    parse(k_catalog, NULL);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_READINGS,
                      parse("{\"type\":\"readings\",\"seq\":812,\"values\":{\"ram_used\":21.3,\"cpu_load\":12,"
                            "\"cpu_temp\":null,\"unknown\":5}}", NULL));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_UINT32(812, s_vals.seq);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(s_vals.has[0]);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_FLOAT(12.0f, s_vals.v[0]);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s_vals.has[1]);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(s_vals.has[2]);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_FLOAT(21.3f, s_vals.v[2]);
    /* a reading left out of a message is no value either */
    parse("{\"type\":\"readings\",\"seq\":813,\"values\":{\"cpu_load\":13}}", NULL);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s_vals.has[2]);
}

static void test_hwinfos_states_by_name(void)
{
    pc_hwinfo_t hw = PC_HW_OK;
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_STATUS, parse("{\"type\":\"status\",\"hwinfo\":\"stale\"}", &hw));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_HW_STALE, hw);
    parse("{\"type\":\"status\",\"hwinfo\":\"shm off\"}", &hw);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_HW_SHM_OFF, hw);
    parse("{\"type\":\"status\",\"hwinfo\":\"something new\"}", &hw);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_HW_UNKNOWN, hw);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("not running", pc_hwinfo_name(PC_HW_NOT_RUNNING));
}

static void test_what_isnt_a_message_is_refused_or_ignored(void)
{
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_INVALID, parse("not json", NULL));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("not JSON", s_err);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_INVALID, parse("{\"type\":\"readings\",\"a\":{\"b\":{\"c\":{\"d\":{}}}}}", NULL));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("nested too deeply", s_err);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_NONE, parse("{\"type\":\"news\",\"text\":\"ignored\"}", NULL)); /* unknown: ignored */
    memset(s_buf, ' ', sizeof(s_buf) - 1);
    s_buf[sizeof(s_buf) - 1] = '\0';
    memcpy(s_buf, "{\"type\":\"status\",\"hwinfo\":\"ok\"}", 31);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_INVALID, pc_parse(s_buf, strlen(s_buf), &s_cat, &s_vals, &(pc_hwinfo_t){ 0 }, s_err,
                                               sizeof(s_err)));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("a frame over 8 KB", s_err);
}

static void test_a_catalogue_beyond_the_rules_is_refused_whole(void)
{
    static const struct {
        const char *json, *why;
    } k_cases[] = {
        { "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[{\"id\":\"CPU\",\"label\":\"x\",\"unit\":\"\"}]}",
          "reading 1: an id is 1-15 characters of a-z, 0-9 and _" },
        { "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[{\"id\":\"a_very_long_identifier\",\"label\":\"x\"}]}",
          "reading 1: an id is 1-15 characters of a-z, 0-9 and _" },
        { "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[{\"id\":\"a\"},{\"id\":\"a\"}]}", "reading 2: id \"a\" is used twice" },
        { "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":{}}", "a catalogue is a list of readings" },
        { "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[{\"id\":\"a\",\"decimals\":4}]}", "reading 1: decimals are 0-3" },
    };
    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(k_cases) / sizeof(k_cases[0]); i++) {
        parse(k_catalog, NULL);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_MESSAGE(PC_MSG_INVALID, parse(k_cases[i].json, NULL), k_cases[i].json);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING(k_cases[i].why, s_err);
        TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(3, s_cat.count); /* the last good one stays */
    }
    char *const end = s_buf + sizeof(s_buf);
    char *p = s_buf + snprintf(s_buf, sizeof(s_buf), "{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[");
    for (int i = 0; i < 33; i++) {
        p += snprintf(p, (size_t)(end - p), "%s{\"id\":\"r%d\"}", i ? "," : "", i);
    }
    strcpy(p, "]}");
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_INVALID, parse(s_buf, NULL));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("32 readings at most", s_err);
}

static void test_long_labels_and_units_are_cut_at_a_character(void)
{
    parse("{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[{\"id\":\"a\",\"label\":\"Teplota procesoru \\u010d\\u00e1st 2\","
          "\"unit\":\"m\\u00b3/h\\u00b2xx\"}]}", NULL);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("Teplota procesoru \xc4\x8d\xc3\xa1s", s_cat.r[0].label); /* 23 bytes */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("m\xc2\xb3/h\xc2\xb2", s_cat.r[0].unit);                  /* 7 bytes */
}

static void test_info_names_the_app_its_protocol_and_hwinfo(void)
{
    pc_info_t info;
    const char *ok = "{\"app\":\"reflbo-stats\",\"protocol\":1,\"version\":\"0.1.0\",\"name\":\"DESKTOP-TEST\",\"hwinfo\":\"ok\"}";
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(pc_parse_info(ok, strlen(ok), &info, s_err, sizeof(s_err)));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(1, info.protocol);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("0.1.0", info.version);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("DESKTOP-TEST", info.name);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_HW_OK, info.hwinfo);
    const char *v2 = "{\"app\":\"reflbo-stats\",\"protocol\":2,\"hwinfo\":\"ok\"}";
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_parse_info(v2, strlen(v2), &info, s_err, sizeof(s_err)));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("protocol 2", s_err);
    const char *other = "{\"app\":\"something else\"}";
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_parse_info(other, strlen(other), &info, s_err, sizeof(s_err)));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("not reflbo-stats", s_err);
}

/* A number no float holds is no value; a sequence or a protocol past an int is clamped, never cast blindly. */
static void test_a_number_no_float_holds_is_no_value(void)
{
    parse(k_catalog, NULL);
    parse("{\"type\":\"readings\",\"seq\":1e20,\"values\":{\"cpu_load\":1e39,\"cpu_temp\":-1e39,"
          "\"ram_used\":3.4e38}}", NULL);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s_vals.has[0]);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s_vals.has[1]);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(s_vals.has[2]);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_UINT32(UINT32_MAX, s_vals.seq);
    parse("{\"type\":\"catalog\",\"readings\":[{\"id\":\"a\",\"min\":-1e39,\"max\":1e39}]}", NULL);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s_cat.r[0].has_min || s_cat.r[0].has_max);
    pc_info_t info;
    const char *big = "{\"app\":\"reflbo-stats\",\"protocol\":1e20}";
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_parse_info(big, strlen(big), &info, s_err, sizeof(s_err)));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("protocol 2147483647", s_err);
}

static void test_the_catalogue_survives_its_file(void)
{
    parse(k_catalog, NULL);
    char out[2048];
    size_t n = pc_catalog_json(&s_cat, out, sizeof(out));
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(n > 0);
    pc_catalog_t back;
    memset(&back, 0, sizeof(back));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_MSG_CATALOG, pc_parse(out, n, &back, &s_vals, &(pc_hwinfo_t){ 0 }, s_err, sizeof(s_err)));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_MEMORY(&s_cat, &back, sizeof(back));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(0, pc_catalog_json(&s_cat, out, 40)); /* too small: nothing */
}

int main(void)
{
    UNITY_BEGIN();
    RUN_TEST(test_the_hello_names_the_protocol_and_the_interval);
    RUN_TEST(test_the_catalogue_is_read_with_its_ranges);
    RUN_TEST(test_readings_go_by_id_and_null_is_no_value);
    RUN_TEST(test_hwinfos_states_by_name);
    RUN_TEST(test_what_isnt_a_message_is_refused_or_ignored);
    RUN_TEST(test_a_catalogue_beyond_the_rules_is_refused_whole);
    RUN_TEST(test_long_labels_and_units_are_cut_at_a_character);
    RUN_TEST(test_info_names_the_app_its_protocol_and_hwinfo);
    RUN_TEST(test_a_number_no_float_holds_is_no_value);
    RUN_TEST(test_the_catalogue_survives_its_file);
    return UNITY_END();
}
```

`components/pcstats/include/pc_msg.h`:

```c
#pragma once

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

/*
 * The PC app's messages (spec §12a, D44): the board's hello, the app's catalogue, readings and status, and
 * GET /v1/info. Pure C on cJSON, host-buildable; the WebSocket and the probe are the device's.
 */

#define PC_PROTOCOL 1
#define PC_READINGS_MAX 32
#define PC_ID_LEN 16    /* 1-15 of a-z, 0-9 and _, and the terminator */
#define PC_LABEL_LEN 24 /* 23 bytes and the terminator */
#define PC_UNIT_LEN 8   /* 7 bytes and the terminator */
#define PC_FRAME_MAX 8192
#define PC_DEPTH_MAX 4
#define PC_INTERVAL_MIN_MS 250
#define PC_INTERVAL_MAX_MS 10000

typedef enum { PC_HW_OK, PC_HW_NOT_RUNNING, PC_HW_SHM_OFF, PC_HW_STALE, PC_HW_UNKNOWN } pc_hwinfo_t;

typedef struct {
    char id[PC_ID_LEN];
    char label[PC_LABEL_LEN];
    char unit[PC_UNIT_LEN];
    uint8_t decimals; /* 0-3 */
    bool has_min, has_max;
    float min, max; /* the usual range, when the app gives one */
} pc_reading_t;

typedef struct {
    uint8_t count;
    pc_reading_t r[PC_READINGS_MAX];
} pc_catalog_t;

/* A readings message's values, in the catalogue's order. */
typedef struct {
    uint32_t seq;
    bool has[PC_READINGS_MAX];
    float v[PC_READINGS_MAX];
} pc_values_t;

typedef struct {
    int protocol;
    char version[16];
    char name[32]; /* the PC's */
    pc_hwinfo_t hwinfo;
} pc_info_t;

typedef enum {
    PC_MSG_INVALID, /* refused, with the reason in err: the connection closes */
    PC_MSG_NONE,    /* a message this firmware doesn't know: ignored */
    PC_MSG_CATALOG,
    PC_MSG_READINGS,
    PC_MSG_STATUS,
} pc_msg_kind_t;

/* {"type":"hello","protocol":1,"interval_ms":N}; the length, or 0 if `size` is too small. */
size_t pc_hello(char *out, size_t size, uint32_t interval_ms);

/* One message from the app. A catalogue replaces `cat` only when all of it is good; readings fill `vals` by the
 * catalogue's ids (an id it lacks is ignored, one left out or null is no value); a status sets `hw`. */
pc_msg_kind_t pc_parse(const char *json, size_t len, pc_catalog_t *cat, pc_values_t *vals, pc_hwinfo_t *hw,
                       char *err, size_t err_size);

/* GET /v1/info's reply; false with the reason for another app or another protocol ("protocol 2"). */
bool pc_parse_info(const char *json, size_t len, pc_info_t *info, char *err, size_t err_size);

/* The catalogue as a catalogue message, for /fs/state/pc.json; the length, or 0 if `size` is too small. */
size_t pc_catalog_json(const pc_catalog_t *cat, char *out, size_t size);

/* The index of the reading with `id`, or -1. */
int pc_catalog_find(const pc_catalog_t *cat, const char *id);

pc_hwinfo_t pc_hwinfo_by_name(const char *name);
const char *pc_hwinfo_name(pc_hwinfo_t hw);
```


`components/pcstats/pc_msg.c`:

```c
#include "pc_msg.h"

#include <float.h>
#include <limits.h>
#include <math.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>

#include "cJSON.h"
#include "util_json.h"

/* The PC app's messages (spec §12a): a catalogue is taken whole or not at all, so the boards' fields never see half
 * of one; anything the protocol doesn't name is ignored. */

static const char *const k_hwinfo[] = { "ok", "not running", "shm off", "stale" }; /* pc_hwinfo_t order */

static pc_msg_kind_t refuse(char *err, size_t size, const char *why)
{
    snprintf(err, size, "%s", why);
    return PC_MSG_INVALID;
}

pc_hwinfo_t pc_hwinfo_by_name(const char *name)
{
    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(k_hwinfo) / sizeof(k_hwinfo[0]); i++) {
        if (name != NULL && strcmp(name, k_hwinfo[i]) == 0) {
            return (pc_hwinfo_t)i;
        }
    }
    return PC_HW_UNKNOWN;
}

const char *pc_hwinfo_name(pc_hwinfo_t hw)
{
    return hw < PC_HW_UNKNOWN ? k_hwinfo[hw] : "unknown";
}

size_t pc_hello(char *out, size_t size, uint32_t interval_ms)
{
    int n = snprintf(out, size, "{\"type\":\"hello\",\"protocol\":%d,\"interval_ms\":%lu}", PC_PROTOCOL,
                     (unsigned long)interval_ms);
    return n > 0 && (size_t)n < size ? (size_t)n : 0;
}

static bool id_ok(const char *id)
{
    size_t n = strlen(id);
    if (n == 0 || n >= PC_ID_LEN) {
        return false;
    }
    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; i++) {
        char c = id[i];
        if (!((c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_')) {
            return false;
        }
    }
    return true;
}

/* `text` into `out` of `size` bytes, cut at a UTF-8 character. */
static void cut(char *out, size_t size, const char *text)
{
    size_t n = strlen(text);
    if (n >= size) {
        n = size - 1;
        while (n > 0 && ((unsigned char)text[n] & 0xC0) == 0x80) {
            n--; /* not inside a character */
        }
    }
    memcpy(out, text, n);
    out[n] = '\0';
}

static const char *text_of(const cJSON *o, const char *key)
{
    const cJSON *item = cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive(o, key);
    return cJSON_IsString(item) ? item->valuestring : "";
}

static bool number_of(const cJSON *o, const char *key, float *out)
{
    const cJSON *item = cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive(o, key);
    if (!cJSON_IsNumber(item) || !isfinite(item->valuedouble) || fabs(item->valuedouble) > FLT_MAX) {
        return false; /* no float holds it: no value, never an infinity */
    }
    *out = (float)item->valuedouble;
    return true;
}

static pc_msg_kind_t parse_catalog(const cJSON *root, pc_catalog_t *cat, char *err, size_t size)
{
    const cJSON *list = cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive(root, "readings");
    if (!cJSON_IsArray(list)) {
        return refuse(err, size, "a catalogue is a list of readings");
    }
    if (cJSON_GetArraySize(list) > PC_READINGS_MAX) {
        return refuse(err, size, "32 readings at most");
    }
    static pc_catalog_t next; /* the app task's alone */
    memset(&next, 0, sizeof(next));
    int i = 0;
    for (const cJSON *e = list->child; e != NULL; e = e->next, i++) {
        pc_reading_t *r = &next.r[i];
        const char *id = text_of(e, "id");
        if (!id_ok(id)) {
            snprintf(err, size, "reading %d: an id is 1-15 characters of a-z, 0-9 and _", i + 1);
            return PC_MSG_INVALID;
        }
        if (pc_catalog_find(&next, id) >= 0) {
            snprintf(err, size, "reading %d: id \"%s\" is used twice", i + 1, id);
            return PC_MSG_INVALID;
        }
        float decimals = 0;
        if (number_of(e, "decimals", &decimals) && (decimals < 0 || decimals > 3 || decimals != floorf(decimals))) {
            snprintf(err, size, "reading %d: decimals are 0-3", i + 1);
            return PC_MSG_INVALID;
        }
        cut(r->id, sizeof(r->id), id);
        cut(r->label, sizeof(r->label), text_of(e, "label"));
        cut(r->unit, sizeof(r->unit), text_of(e, "unit"));
        r->decimals = (uint8_t)decimals;
        r->has_min = number_of(e, "min", &r->min);
        r->has_max = number_of(e, "max", &r->max);
        next.count = (uint8_t)(i + 1);
    }
    *cat = next;
    return PC_MSG_CATALOG;
}

static pc_msg_kind_t parse_readings(const cJSON *root, const pc_catalog_t *cat, pc_values_t *vals)
{
    const cJSON *values = cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive(root, "values");
    const cJSON *seq = cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive(root, "seq");
    memset(vals, 0, sizeof(*vals));
    double n = cJSON_IsNumber(seq) && seq->valuedouble >= 0 ? seq->valuedouble : 0; /* NaN too */
    vals->seq = n >= (double)UINT32_MAX ? UINT32_MAX : (uint32_t)n;
    for (int i = 0; i < cat->count; i++) {
        vals->has[i] = number_of(values, cat->r[i].id, &vals->v[i]); /* null or left out: no value */
    }
    return PC_MSG_READINGS;
}

pc_msg_kind_t pc_parse(const char *json, size_t len, pc_catalog_t *cat, pc_values_t *vals, pc_hwinfo_t *hw,
                       char *err, size_t err_size)
{
    if (len > PC_FRAME_MAX) {
        return refuse(err, err_size, "a frame over 8 KB");
    }
    if (util_json_depth(json) > PC_DEPTH_MAX) {
        return refuse(err, err_size, "nested too deeply");
    }
    cJSON *root = cJSON_ParseWithLength(json, len);
    if (!cJSON_IsObject(root)) {
        cJSON_Delete(root);
        return refuse(err, err_size, "not JSON");
    }
    const char *type = text_of(root, "type");
    pc_msg_kind_t kind = PC_MSG_NONE;
    if (strcmp(type, "catalog") == 0) {
        kind = parse_catalog(root, cat, err, err_size);
    } else if (strcmp(type, "readings") == 0) {
        kind = parse_readings(root, cat, vals);
    } else if (strcmp(type, "status") == 0) {
        *hw = pc_hwinfo_by_name(text_of(root, "hwinfo"));
        kind = PC_MSG_STATUS;
    }
    cJSON_Delete(root);
    return kind;
}

bool pc_parse_info(const char *json, size_t len, pc_info_t *info, char *err, size_t err_size)
{
    memset(info, 0, sizeof(*info));
    if (util_json_depth(json) > PC_DEPTH_MAX) {
        refuse(err, err_size, "nested too deeply");
        return false;
    }
    cJSON *root = cJSON_ParseWithLength(json, len);
    bool ok = false;
    if (!cJSON_IsObject(root) || strcmp(text_of(root, "app"), "reflbo-stats") != 0) {
        refuse(err, err_size, "not reflbo-stats");
    } else {
        const cJSON *protocol = cJSON_GetObjectItemCaseSensitive(root, "protocol");
        double p = cJSON_IsNumber(protocol) && isfinite(protocol->valuedouble) ? protocol->valuedouble : 0;
        info->protocol = p >= INT_MAX ? INT_MAX : p <= INT_MIN ? INT_MIN : (int)p;
        cut(info->version, sizeof(info->version), text_of(root, "version"));
        cut(info->name, sizeof(info->name), text_of(root, "name"));
        info->hwinfo = pc_hwinfo_by_name(text_of(root, "hwinfo"));
        if (info->protocol != PC_PROTOCOL) {
            snprintf(err, err_size, "protocol %d", info->protocol);
        } else {
            ok = true;
        }
    }
    cJSON_Delete(root);
    return ok;
}

size_t pc_catalog_json(const pc_catalog_t *cat, char *out, size_t size)
{
    cJSON *root = cJSON_CreateObject();
    cJSON_AddStringToObject(root, "type", "catalog");
    cJSON *list = cJSON_AddArrayToObject(root, "readings");
    for (int i = 0; i < cat->count; i++) {
        const pc_reading_t *r = &cat->r[i];
        cJSON *e = cJSON_CreateObject();
        cJSON_AddStringToObject(e, "id", r->id);
        cJSON_AddStringToObject(e, "label", r->label);
        cJSON_AddStringToObject(e, "unit", r->unit);
        cJSON_AddNumberToObject(e, "decimals", r->decimals);
        if (r->has_min) {
            cJSON_AddNumberToObject(e, "min", r->min);
        }
        if (r->has_max) {
            cJSON_AddNumberToObject(e, "max", r->max);
        }
        cJSON_AddItemToArray(list, e);
    }
    bool ok = cJSON_PrintPreallocated(root, out, (int)size, false);
    cJSON_Delete(root);
    return ok ? strlen(out) : 0;
}

int pc_catalog_find(const pc_catalog_t *cat, const char *id)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < cat->count; i++) {
        if (strcmp(cat->r[i].id, id) == 0) {
            return i;
        }
    }
    return -1;
}
```

### Task 2: The session's rules (`pcstats`)

**Files:**
- Create: `components/pcstats/include/pc_session.h`, `components/pcstats/pc_session.c`, `test/host/test_pc_session.c`
- Modify: `components/pcstats/CMakeLists.txt`, `test/host/CMakeLists.txt`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (`pc_session.h`): `pc_session_options_t` {`auto_always`, `preset`}, `pc_session_t`, `pc_start_t` {`to_always`, `select`}, `pc_end_t` {`restore_mode`, `mode`, `select`}, `pc_check_t` (`PC_STEADY`, `PC_RECONNECT`, `PC_END`), and
  - `void pc_session_init(pc_session_t *)`;
  - `bool pc_session_may_start(const pc_session_options_t *, bool in_always)`;
  - `pc_start_t pc_session_start(pc_session_t *, const pc_session_options_t *, bool in_always, int mode, const char *shown, int64_t now_ms)`;
  - `void pc_session_heard(pc_session_t *, int64_t now_ms)`;
  - `pc_check_t pc_session_check(pc_session_t *, uint32_t interval_ms, int64_t now_ms)`;
  - `pc_end_t pc_session_end(pc_session_t *, bool still_always, const char *shown)`;
  - `bool pc_session_pauses_cycle(const pc_session_t *, const char *shown)`, `bool pc_session_keeps_wifi(const pc_session_t *)`;
  - `int pc_session_saved_mode(const pc_session_t *s, int mode)`, `const char *pc_session_saved_preset(const pc_session_t *s, const char *shown)`: what the files keep while a session runs;
  - `void pc_session_defer_night(pc_session_t *s, int64_t until)`, `int64_t pc_session_take_night(pc_session_t *s, int64_t now)`: a schedule's night that comes during a session;
  - `PC_SESSION_ALWAYS` (2), sync mode `always` as `settings.h` numbers it (`main/app_pc.c` asserts it).

Spec §12a.4 as pure logic, so every case is a host test:
- a session switches to sync mode `always` and to its preset, each when asked;
- without `auto_always` it starts only in `always`, as a session elsewhere would end with the sync;
- silence brings one reconnect after three intervals (5 s at least), and the end about 15 s after the last message (twice the first wait when that is longer);
- the end gives back the mode only while it is still the session's `always`, and the preset only while the session's own is on screen ("your hand wins"); presets go by id;
- the files never keep the session's switches: while it runs, the mode to save is the one before the session's `always`, and the active preset to save the one before its preset while that is on screen, so a reboot comes back as the owner left it;
- a schedule's night during a session waits for its end: the latest night's end is kept, and the session's end hands it over while it is still ahead.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests.**

`test/host/test_pc_session.c`:

```c
#include <string.h>

#include "pc_session.h"
#include "unity.h"

/* The PC session's rules (spec §12a.4, D44): what a session switches when it starts, when silence means a
 * reconnect and when the end, and what comes back then: only what the owner hasn't changed by hand. */

#define MODE_TIMES 0
#define MODE_INTERVAL 1
#define MODE_ALWAYS 2

static pc_session_t s;
static const pc_session_options_t k_both = { .auto_always = true, .preset = "pc" };

void setUp(void)
{
    pc_session_init(&s);
}

void tearDown(void) {}

static void test_a_session_switches_to_always_and_the_pc_preset(void)
{
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(pc_session_may_start(&k_both, MODE_TIMES == MODE_ALWAYS));
    pc_start_t a = pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(a.to_always);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("pc", a.select);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(s.active);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(pc_session_keeps_wifi(&s));
}

static void test_the_end_brings_back_the_mode_and_the_preset(void)
{
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    pc_end_t e = pc_session_end(&s, true, "pc");
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(e.restore_mode);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(MODE_TIMES, e.mode);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("weather", e.select);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(s.active);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_session_keeps_wifi(&s));
}

static void test_a_preset_chosen_by_hand_stays(void)
{
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    pc_end_t e = pc_session_end(&s, true, "rain"); /* KEY moved on from the PC preset */
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(e.restore_mode);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("", e.select);
}

static void test_always_turned_off_by_hand_stays_off(void)
{
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    pc_end_t e = pc_session_end(&s, false, "pc"); /* BOOT double left always */
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(e.restore_mode);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("weather", e.select);
}

static void test_the_owners_own_always_isnt_undone(void)
{
    pc_start_t a = pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, true, MODE_ALWAYS, "energy", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(a.to_always);
    pc_end_t e = pc_session_end(&s, true, "pc");
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(e.restore_mode);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("energy", e.select);
}

static void test_without_a_preset_nothing_is_selected_or_restored(void)
{
    const pc_session_options_t only_always = { .auto_always = true, .preset = "" };
    pc_start_t a = pc_session_start(&s, &only_always, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("", a.select);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("", pc_session_end(&s, true, "weather").select);
}

static void test_a_pc_preset_already_on_screen_isnt_restored_to_itself(void)
{
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "pc", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("", pc_session_end(&s, true, "pc").select);
}

static void test_without_switching_to_always_a_session_needs_always(void)
{
    const pc_session_options_t preset_only = { .auto_always = false, .preset = "pc" };
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_session_may_start(&preset_only, false)); /* it would end with the sync */
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(pc_session_may_start(&preset_only, true));
    pc_start_t a = pc_session_start(&s, &preset_only, true, MODE_ALWAYS, "weather", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(a.to_always);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("pc", a.select);
}

static void test_the_cycle_pauses_while_the_session_preset_shows(void)
{
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_session_pauses_cycle(&s, "pc")); /* no session */
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_TRUE(pc_session_pauses_cycle(&s, "pc"));
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(pc_session_pauses_cycle(&s, "rain"));
}

static void test_silence_brings_a_reconnect_then_the_end(void)
{
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 0);
    pc_session_heard(&s, 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_STEADY, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 5999)); /* 1 s interval: quiet for 5 s at least */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_RECONNECT, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 6001));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_STEADY, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 8000)); /* reconnecting: once */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_END, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 16001));    /* about 15 s after the PC went quiet */
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 0);
    pc_session_heard(&s, 1000);
    pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 6001);
    pc_session_heard(&s, 7000);                                         /* the reconnect worked */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_RECONNECT, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 12001)); /* a new silence gets its own reconnect */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_STEADY, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 16001));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_END, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 22001));
}

static void test_a_slow_interval_waits_longer(void)
{
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 0);
    pc_session_heard(&s, 0);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_STEADY, pc_session_check(&s, 10000, 29999)); /* three intervals of 10 s */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_RECONNECT, pc_session_check(&s, 10000, 30001));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_STEADY, pc_session_check(&s, 10000, 59999));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_END, pc_session_check(&s, 10000, 60001));
}

/* What the files keep while a session runs: the mode and the preset from before, so a restart in the middle of one
 * gives back what the owner had (spec §12a.4). NULL: the preset as it is. */
static void test_the_files_keep_what_the_owner_had(void)
{
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(MODE_TIMES, pc_session_saved_mode(&s, MODE_TIMES)); /* no session: as it is */
    TEST_ASSERT_NULL(pc_session_saved_preset(&s, "pc"));
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(MODE_TIMES, pc_session_saved_mode(&s, MODE_ALWAYS)); /* the session's `always` isn't saved */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("weather", pc_session_saved_preset(&s, "pc"));
    TEST_ASSERT_NULL(pc_session_saved_preset(&s, "rain")); /* KEY moved on: the owner's choice is saved */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(MODE_INTERVAL, pc_session_saved_mode(&s, MODE_INTERVAL)); /* a mode chosen by hand */
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, true, MODE_ALWAYS, "pc", 1000); /* the owner's own `always`, the PC preset shown */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(MODE_ALWAYS, pc_session_saved_mode(&s, MODE_ALWAYS));
    TEST_ASSERT_NULL(pc_session_saved_preset(&s, "pc"));
}

/* A schedule's night that comes due during a session starts at its end, while the night's own end is still to come
 * (spec §12a.4). */
static void test_a_night_due_during_a_session_comes_at_its_end(void)
{
    pc_session_defer_night(&s, 1000000); /* no session: nothing is kept */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_INT64(0, pc_session_take_night(&s, 900000));
    pc_session_start(&s, &k_both, false, MODE_TIMES, "weather", 1000);
    pc_session_defer_night(&s, 1000000); /* 23:00, until 06:00 */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_INT64(1000000, pc_session_take_night(&s, 900000));
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_INT64(0, pc_session_take_night(&s, 900000)); /* once */
    pc_session_defer_night(&s, 1000000);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_INT64(0, pc_session_take_night(&s, 1000000)); /* its end has come: none */
    pc_session_defer_night(&s, 2000000);
    pc_session_defer_night(&s, 1500000); /* two: the one that ends last */
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_INT64(2000000, pc_session_take_night(&s, 900000));
}

static void test_no_session_has_nothing_to_check(void)
{
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL(PC_STEADY, pc_session_check(&s, 1000, 1000000));
    pc_end_t e = pc_session_end(&s, true, "pc");
    TEST_ASSERT_FALSE(e.restore_mode);
    TEST_ASSERT_EQUAL_STRING("", e.select);
}

int main(void)
{
    UNITY_BEGIN();
    RUN_TEST(test_a_session_switches_to_always_and_the_pc_preset);
    RUN_TEST(test_the_end_brings_back_the_mode_and_the_preset);
    RUN_TEST(test_a_preset_chosen_by_hand_stays);
    RUN_TEST(test_always_turned_off_by_hand_stays_off);
    RUN_TEST(test_the_owners_own_always_isnt_undone);
    RUN_TEST(test_without_a_preset_nothing_is_selected_or_restored);
    RUN_TEST(test_a_pc_preset_already_on_screen_isnt_restored_to_itself);
    RUN_TEST(test_without_switching_to_always_a_session_needs_always);
    RUN_TEST(test_the_cycle_pauses_while_the_session_preset_shows);
    RUN_TEST(test_silence_brings_a_reconnect_then_the_end);
    RUN_TEST(test_a_slow_interval_waits_longer);
    RUN_TEST(test_the_files_keep_what_the_owner_had);
    RUN_TEST(test_a_night_due_during_a_session_comes_at_its_end);
    RUN_TEST(test_no_session_has_nothing_to_check);
    return UNITY_END();
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the rules.**

`components/pcstats/include/pc_session.h`:

```c
#pragma once

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdint.h>

/*
 * The PC session's rules (spec §12a.4, D44), pure: what a session switches when a sync finds the PC, when silence
 * means a reconnect and when the end, and what comes back then. The owner's hand wins: a mode or preset changed
 * by hand since the start stays. Presets go by id, so a presets save meanwhile doesn't move them. Host-buildable.
 */

#define PC_PRESET_ID_LEN 16 /* as UI_PRESET_ID_LEN */
#define PC_SESSION_ALWAYS 2 /* sync mode `always`: SETTINGS_SYNC_ALWAYS (main/app_pc.c checks) */

typedef struct {
    bool auto_always;               /* switch to sync mode always */
    char preset[PC_PRESET_ID_LEN]; /* show this preset; "" for none */
} pc_session_options_t;

typedef struct {
    bool active;
    bool set_always;  /* the session switched to always, from mode_before */
    int mode_before;
    char preset[PC_PRESET_ID_LEN];        /* the preset the session showed; "" for none */
    char preset_before[PC_PRESET_ID_LEN]; /* the one on screen before it; "" when it was the session's own */
    int64_t heard_ms;                     /* the last message, or the start */
    bool reconnecting;
    int64_t night_until;                  /* a schedule's night that came due meanwhile, until then (UTC); 0: none */
} pc_session_t;

typedef struct {
    bool to_always;
    char select[PC_PRESET_ID_LEN]; /* "" for none */
} pc_start_t;

typedef struct {
    bool restore_mode;
    int mode;                      /* when restore_mode */
    char select[PC_PRESET_ID_LEN]; /* "" for none */
} pc_end_t;

typedef enum { PC_STEADY, PC_RECONNECT, PC_END } pc_check_t;

void pc_session_init(pc_session_t *s);
/* Without switching to always, a session lasts only while Wi-Fi stays up anyway: in always. */
bool pc_session_may_start(const pc_session_options_t *o, bool in_always);
pc_start_t pc_session_start(pc_session_t *s, const pc_session_options_t *o, bool in_always, int mode,
                            const char *shown, int64_t now_ms);
void pc_session_heard(pc_session_t *s, int64_t now_ms);
/* After three intervals without a message (5 s at least): a reconnect, once; after 15 s, or twice the first
 * wait if that's longer: the end. */
pc_check_t pc_session_check(pc_session_t *s, uint32_t interval_ms, int64_t now_ms);
/* `still_always`: the sync mode is still always; `shown`: the preset on screen now. */
pc_end_t pc_session_end(pc_session_t *s, bool still_always, const char *shown);
bool pc_session_pauses_cycle(const pc_session_t *s, const char *shown);
/* Quiet hours and a night's schedule entry don't end a running session (spec §12a.4). */
bool pc_session_keeps_wifi(const pc_session_t *s);
/* What the files keep while a session runs, so a restart in the middle of one gives back what the owner had: the mode
 * from before while the mode is still the session's `always` (`mode` otherwise), and the preset from before while
 * the session's own is on screen (NULL: the one shown). */
int pc_session_saved_mode(const pc_session_t *s, int mode);
const char *pc_session_saved_preset(const pc_session_t *s, const char *shown);
/* A schedule's night that comes due during a session is kept for its end (the one that ends last, if two)... */
void pc_session_defer_night(pc_session_t *s, int64_t until);
/* ...which starts it while its own end is still to come: `until`, once, or 0. */
int64_t pc_session_take_night(pc_session_t *s, int64_t now);
```


`components/pcstats/pc_session.c`:

```c
#include "pc_session.h"

#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>

#define QUIET_AT_LEAST_MS 5000
#define END_AT_LEAST_MS 15000

static void copy_id(char *out, const char *id)
{
    snprintf(out, PC_PRESET_ID_LEN, "%s", id != NULL ? id : "");
}

void pc_session_init(pc_session_t *s)
{
    memset(s, 0, sizeof(*s));
}

bool pc_session_may_start(const pc_session_options_t *o, bool in_always)
{
    return o->auto_always || in_always;
}

pc_start_t pc_session_start(pc_session_t *s, const pc_session_options_t *o, bool in_always, int mode,
                            const char *shown, int64_t now_ms)
{
    pc_start_t a = { 0 };
    pc_session_init(s);
    s->active = true;
    s->heard_ms = now_ms;
    s->mode_before = mode;
    if (o->auto_always && !in_always) {
        a.to_always = s->set_always = true;
    }
    copy_id(s->preset, o->preset);
    if (s->preset[0] != '\0' && strcmp(s->preset, shown) != 0) {
        copy_id(a.select, s->preset);
        copy_id(s->preset_before, shown);
    }
    return a;
}

void pc_session_heard(pc_session_t *s, int64_t now_ms)
{
    s->heard_ms = now_ms;
    s->reconnecting = false;
}

pc_check_t pc_session_check(pc_session_t *s, uint32_t interval_ms, int64_t now_ms)
{
    if (!s->active) {
        return PC_STEADY;
    }
    int64_t quiet = (int64_t)interval_ms * 3;
    quiet = quiet > QUIET_AT_LEAST_MS ? quiet : QUIET_AT_LEAST_MS;
    int64_t end = quiet * 2 > END_AT_LEAST_MS ? quiet * 2 : END_AT_LEAST_MS;
    int64_t silent = now_ms - s->heard_ms;
    if (silent > end) {
        return PC_END;
    }
    if (silent > quiet && !s->reconnecting) {
        s->reconnecting = true;
        return PC_RECONNECT;
    }
    return PC_STEADY;
}

pc_end_t pc_session_end(pc_session_t *s, bool still_always, const char *shown)
{
    pc_end_t e = { 0 };
    if (!s->active) {
        return e;
    }
    if (s->set_always && still_always) {
        e.restore_mode = true;
        e.mode = s->mode_before;
    }
    if (s->preset_before[0] != '\0' && strcmp(shown, s->preset) == 0) {
        copy_id(e.select, s->preset_before);
    }
    pc_session_init(s);
    return e;
}

bool pc_session_pauses_cycle(const pc_session_t *s, const char *shown)
{
    return s->active && s->preset[0] != '\0' && strcmp(s->preset, shown) == 0;
}

bool pc_session_keeps_wifi(const pc_session_t *s)
{
    return s->active;
}

int pc_session_saved_mode(const pc_session_t *s, int mode)
{
    return s->active && s->set_always && mode == PC_SESSION_ALWAYS ? s->mode_before : mode;
}

const char *pc_session_saved_preset(const pc_session_t *s, const char *shown)
{
    return s->active && s->preset_before[0] != '\0' && strcmp(shown, s->preset) == 0 ? s->preset_before : NULL;
}

void pc_session_defer_night(pc_session_t *s, int64_t until)
{
    if (s->active && until > s->night_until) {
        s->night_until = until;
    }
}

int64_t pc_session_take_night(pc_session_t *s, int64_t now)
{
    int64_t until = s->night_until > now ? s->night_until : 0;
    s->night_until = 0;
    return until;
}
```

### Task 3: The presets in a block of their own in RTC FAST memory (`main`)

**Files:**
- Modify: `main/app.c`, `main/app_internal.h`, `main/app_ui.c`, `main/app_menu.c`, `main/app_web.c`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `app_ui_state_t` without its `presets`; `ui_presets_t *app_presets(void)` is the one place they live (`app_ui.c`'s `s_presets`); `void app_ui_export_presets(ui_presets_t *)`, `void app_ui_import_presets(const ui_presets_t *)`; in `app.c` the block `s_presets_snap` in `RTC_FAST_ATTR` with its own magic (`PRESETS_MAGIC`) and version (`PRESETS_VERSION` 1), sealed with the snapshot before a deep sleep, and a warm wake that needs both valid.

Why: RTC slow memory holds the deep-sleep snapshot, which D42's titles brought to 6 832 bytes of its 7 KB cap; M7's PC ids and visuals don't fit there. The presets, with everything M7 adds to them, fit RTC FAST memory whole, which `CONFIG_ESP_SYSTEM_ALLOW_RTC_FAST_MEM_AS_HEAP=y` keeps powered in deep sleep; the ESP32-S3's RTC FAST memory is reachable from both cores. A block of their own keeps the snapshot well under its cap for M8's values.

No new host test: nothing pure changes, and the firmware's build is the check (Step 2). The board's checks (Task 14) cover a deep sleep's warm wake.

- [ ] **Step 1: Move the presets.**

### Task 4: A number for every numeric field (`ui`)

**Files:**
- Modify: `components/ui/include/ui_fields.h`, `components/ui/ui_internal.h`, `components/ui/ui_fields.c`, `components/ui/ui_forecast.c`, `components/ui/ui_solar.c`, `test/host/test_ui_fields.c`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: in `ui_value_t`, `bool has_num; float num; bool has_range; float range_min, range_max;` and in `ui_internal.h` `ui_set_num(ui_value_t *, float)` and `ui_set_range(ui_value_t *, float lo, float hi)`.

The visuals (Tasks 7 and 8) draw a number, not a text, between a minimum and a maximum (spec §5.3). Each resolver says it in the field's base unit, whatever its text's unit: °C or °F as set, %, kW for a power and kWh for an energy (their text switches to W under 1 kW, D38), µg/m³; signed, so a power to the grid is negative. The fields with a range of their own say it: 0–100 for a humidity, a battery, own use. The chart and the flow, the day, the Moon and the texts carry none.
